</ul>
<p><strong>Proof of small computation overhead of calibration:</strong> </p>
<ul>
<li>5 calibration iterations for each <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="177" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> training iteration (as in our experiments). For the sake of simplicity, our linear layer: <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="178" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D466 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D465 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c22C5"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msup space="3"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: 0.363em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D447 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msup></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>⋅</mo><msup><mi>w</mi><mi>T</mi></msup></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> with <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="179" style="font-size: 121.3%; margin-left: -0.045em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" style="font-size: 77.7%;"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D466 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-mrow></mjx-den></mjx-row></mjx-dtable></mjx-dbox></mjx-frac></mjx-mfrac></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mo>/</mo></mrow><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>30</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>⋅</mo><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>⋅</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mo>/</mo></mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>⋅</mo><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1200</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> ~ <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="185" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mfrac><mjx-frac><mjx-num><mjx-nstrut></mjx-nstrut><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-num><mjx-dbox><mjx-dtable><mjx-line></mjx-line><mjx-row><mjx-den><mjx-dstrut></mjx-dstrut><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c34"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-den></mjx-row></mjx-dtable></mjx-dbox></mjx-frac></mjx-mfrac><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43F TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3C"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D456 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45B TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D436 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.045em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D465 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2C"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msub space="2"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D436 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.045em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D466 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>40</mn></mfrac><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container></li>
</ul>
<p>Therefore, FLOP of calibration is small to FLOP of training (Eg. in Tab.1, INSTANT-5 with reported 475M training FLOPs on EfficientFormerL1 <em>(line 351 main paper)</em> requires extra 37 MFLOPs for calibration)</p>
<p>Therefore, only reporting training flop still reflects a fair comparison with other methods. However, this calibration FLOPs is small but not negligible. Thank the Reviewer for this suggestion. We added this analysis to the main paper to make our method clearer <em>(line 419, line 810 of main paper)</em>.</p>
<p><em><strong>→ “Q3: Given that the subspace is recalibrated every&nbsp; steps, would "periodically updated" or "cached" be a more accurate description than "static"?”</strong></em></p>
<p>Yes, “periodically updated” is more accurate. Our purpose of writing “static” is to indicate the slow change of the compression tensor over time (static over <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="186" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> training iterations). However, “static” is not exactly accurate. We appropriately thank the reviewer for their suggestion. We changed this in the main text.</p>
<p><em><strong>→ “Q4: The oversampling parameter&nbsp; appears critical for performance (Fig. 6, Fig. 8). How sensitive is the method to the choice of p and the calibration frequency? Does this introduce a significant hyperparameter tuning burden?”</strong></em></p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Regarding to oversampling hyperparameter <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="187" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container></strong>, with small <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="188" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2C"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="2"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2C"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="2"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2C"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="2"><mjx-c class="mjx-c33"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>, the subspace selection varies significantly so the performance changes much. For example, due to low-rank activation and gradient, subspaces <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="189" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D443 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> (or <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="190" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D444 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>) with 1 vector can achieve good approximation (reach <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="191" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D716 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c2E"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c39"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c35"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.95</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>) (as shown in Figure 11 in main paper). Oversampling with <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="192" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c35"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> will result to subspaces with 6 vectors, which significantly affect the performance. When <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="193" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> becomes larger, they do not capture the core subspace more so performance does not change much (subspace with 6 vectors <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="194" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c35"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> is comparable to subspace with 11 vectors <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="195" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>). We conducted experiments to see the sensitivity of <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="196" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. As shown in our Figure.6, <em>(line 445)</em> and Figure.8b <em>(line 1095)</em>, the performance tends to become saturated when increasing <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="197" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. Therefore, with small <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="198" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>, INSTANT is sensitive to that value, but if the resource we have is bigger, <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="199" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> is not so sensitive.</li>
<li><strong>Regarding to calibration frequency</strong>, smaller <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="200" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> (update subspace more frequently) is better as shown in the Appendix. However, smaller <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="201" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> results in more calibration overhead, and its computational cost may not be negligible anymore. To find an appropriate calibration frequency, we do empirical study as shown in Tab.1 <em>(line 340)</em> , Tab.2 <em>(line 378)</em> and Figure 8 <em>(line 1095)</em>.</li>
</ul>
<p>In short, these choices of <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="202" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> and <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="203" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> depend on resource we want. Like other hyperparameters, we need to tune to find the appropriate numbers. However, these choices are stable within task, e.g the choices of <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="204" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c35"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>, <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="205" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> perform well in 5 image datasets.</p>
</div></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-odd" data-id="BHK1EUWq8O"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><span>Official Review of Submission6348 by Reviewer 9WbD</span></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制笔记 BHK1EUWq8O 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note BHK1EUWq8O"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note BHK1EUWq8O</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(255, 187, 187); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Review</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Reviewer 9WbD</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>29 Oct 2025, 11:44 (modified: 29 Nov 2025, 03:52)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=BHK1EUWq8O">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Summary:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>This paper proposes a new distillation technique called grafting, aimed at improving the generalization of autoregressive language models. The grafting strategy integrates sequence trees generated at multiple temperatures into a single distillation target. The paper emphasizes the balance between model compression and generalization, addressing the mode-covering behavior crucial for building more generalizable models. The experimental validation demonstrates the method's effectiveness across several datasets, particularly CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Soundness:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Presentation:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Contribution:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">2: fair</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Strengths:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>Originality: The grafting strategy is an original approach for balancing model compression and generalization in language model distillation.</p>
<p>Experimental Validation: The method is validated across several datasets, and the results show some potential for improving model performance while reducing computational and memory costs.</p>
<p>Clarity: The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the experimental setup and methodology.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Weaknesses:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>Lack of Comparison with Related Work: While the paper mentions the error accumulation problem in other activation compression methods, it does not provide a sufficient comparison with existing methods like Sakr &amp; Khailany’s ESPACE or Yang et al.’s LBP-WHT in terms of accuracy and performance. Without this comparison, the claims about grafting’s effectiveness remain unclear.</p>
<p>Limited Experimental Setup: The experiments are mainly focused on simple image classification tasks (e.g., CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100). These datasets may not fully demonstrate the method's potential in more complex or real-world scenarios.</p>
<p>Unaddressed Limitations: The paper does not sufficiently explore some of the limitations of the proposed method, including the computational cost of SVD and its scalability to larger models or long sequences.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Questions:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>Could you provide a more detailed comparison between grafting and existing methods like Sakr &amp; Khailany’s ESPACE or Yang et al.’s LBP-WHT, particularly in terms of accuracy and computational efficiency?</p>
<p>How do you plan to scale the grafting method for larger models or longer sequences, and what are the potential challenges?</p>
<p>Could you provide further experiments on more complex datasets or real-world tasks to validate the method’s generalizability?</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Flag For Ethics Review:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">No ethics review needed.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Rating:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">4: marginally below the acceptance threshold. But would not mind if paper is accepted</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Confidence:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: You are fairly confident in your assessment. It is possible that you did not understand some parts of the submission or that you are unfamiliar with some pieces of related work. Math/other details were not carefully checked.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Code Of Conduct:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">Yes</span></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div><div class="note-replies"><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="3JMl3xTTeF"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><strong>Restatement of existing experiments and extra baselines for comparisons (Response 1/n)</strong></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制注释 3JMl3xTTeF 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note 3JMl3xTTeF"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note 3JMl3xTTeF</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>21 Nov 2025, 03:25 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:38)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=3JMl3xTTeF">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>Thank you for the feedback. We would like to kindly point out that our paper presents a compression method that projects gradients and activations into a low-rank subspace and performs computation within these compressed representations. As such, we neither perform distillation nor grafting. We also do not present a "grafting strategy integrates sequence trees generated at multiple temperatures into a single distillation target" .</p>
<p><em><strong>→ W1 &amp; Q1. Could you provide a more detailed comparison between grafting and existing methods like Sakr &amp; Khailany’s ESPACE or Yang et al.’s LBP-WHT, particularly in terms of accuracy and computational efficiency?</strong></em></p>
<p>Please note that we are not performing ‘grafting’. In terms of comparison to related methods, such as LBP-WHT, we kindly refer the reviewer to Section 4 <em>(line 270, main paper)</em>. Since LBP-WHT is our main competitor, we provided a detailed comparison between our method and LBP-WHT in terms of both accuracy and computational efficiency <em>(Tab.1, line 340 and Tab.2 line 378)</em>.</p>
<p>Regarding ESPACE, we would also like to clarify why ESPACE was not included in our original set of baselines. ESPACE primarily focuses on compressing activations only <em>(line 96-100 main paper)</em>, whereas our main objective is to jointly compress both activations and activation gradients. For this reason, ESPACE targets a somewhat different problem setting from ours, which is why it was not selected as a main comparison in the initial submission. However, we provide an extra comparison with ESPACE below. We also provide extra baseline: <em>Random -</em> which is INSTANT using random subspaces <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="206" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D443 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2C"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" space="2"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D444 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Q</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>  for compressing activation <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="207" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D465 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> and activation gradient <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="208" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D466 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> to highlight the importance and efficiency of our calibration.</p>

<p>The experimental results demonstrate that INSTANT significantly outperforms both ESPACE and the <em>Random</em> projection method, where the compression rank is set to <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="209" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mfrac><mjx-frac><mjx-num><mjx-nstrut></mjx-nstrut><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-num><mjx-dbox><mjx-dtable><mjx-line></mjx-line><mjx-row><mjx-den><mjx-dstrut></mjx-dstrut><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-den></mjx-row></mjx-dtable></mjx-dbox></mjx-frac></mjx-mfrac></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> or <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="210" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mfrac><mjx-frac><mjx-num><mjx-nstrut></mjx-nstrut><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-num><mjx-dbox><mjx-dtable><mjx-line></mjx-line><mjx-row><mjx-den><mjx-dstrut></mjx-dstrut><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c34"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-den></mjx-row></mjx-dtable></mjx-dbox></mjx-frac></mjx-mfrac></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>N</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> , with <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="211" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> representing the input dimension. This highlights that INSTANT effectively requires a compact yet precise low-rank space to retain gradient information for accurate backpropagation through the network. Notably, in the case of full fine-tuning, the <em>Random</em> projection method fails to converge, as the gradients struggle to propagate through multiple layers, resulting in significant error accumulation due to the lack of a well-defined low-rank space. These extra experimental results are updated to the main paper <em>(line 1338, line 1377).</em></p>
<p>Moreover, understanding the concerns about the comparison between our method and other optimizer gradient compression methods (GaLore, CompAct), we also add extra baselines in the Appendix H <em>(line 1129)</em> and analyze the strengths, weaknesses of these methods, and suggest the combination of INSTANT with these methods.</p>

background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="7AKjFmz4lb"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="parent-title"><h5><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-share-alt " aria-hidden="true"></span> Replying to Restatement of existing experiments and extra baselines for comparisons (Response 1/n)</h5></div><div class="heading"><h4><strong>(Continue) Restatement of existing experiments and extra baselines for comparisons (Response 2/n)</strong></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制备注 7AKjFmz4lb 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note 7AKjFmz4lb"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note 7AKjFmz4lb</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>21 Nov 2025, 03:27 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:38)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=7AKjFmz4lb">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p><em><strong>→ W2. Limited Experimental Setup: The experiments are mainly focused on simple image classification tasks (e.g., CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100). These datasets may not fully demonstrate the method's potential in more complex or real-world scenarios.</strong></em></p>
<p>In our experimental setup, we do not restrict ourselves to simple image classification tasks. We also conduct extensive experiments on BERT-like models using the GLUE benchmark to evaluate performance on natural language processing tasks <em>(Tab.2 line 378)</em>. These benchmarks, both for vision and natural language processing, are widely adopted in the community for assessing compression methods, and many prior works—such as LBP-WHT, PocketEngine, On-Device Training, LoRA, and GaLore—use the same evaluation protocols.</p>
<p><em><strong>→ W4 &amp; Q2. How do you plan to scale the grafting method for larger models or longer sequences, and what are the potential challenges?</strong></em></p>
<p>In Appendix L <em>(line 1373)</em>, we conduct additional experiments to evaluate the behavior of INSTANT on larger models such as ViT-B/32 <em>(line 1385)</em> and TinyLlama <em>(line 1433)</em>. The results indicate that, on ViT-B/32, INSTANT achieves performance comparable to standard training while reducing both activation memory and computational cost by a factor of 5. For TinyLlama, the performance gap is larger; however, our method still yields substantial savings in activation memory and computational complexity. </p>
<p>While the results are encouraging, we identify some potential challenges that we believe should be addressed in future work to further scale INSTANT:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Error accumulation in deeper models.</strong> As model depth increases, error stacking may become more pronounced. This could necessitate more frequent calibration updates or the use of a larger energy threshold and oversampling factor in order to reliably capture an appropriate low-rank subspace.</li>
<li><strong>Limited calibration for very large models.</strong> As shown in Appendix L.4 <em>(line 1434)</em>, when scaling to models with around one billion parameters, the performance gap between INSTANT and vanilla training widens. An explanation is that large models have higher capacity, so a small number of calibration batches may not provide sufficiently rich statistics to construct an accurate low-rank projection, ultimately making it harder to capture the optimal low-rank space.</li>
</ul>
<p><em><strong>→ Q3. Could you provide further experiments on more complex datasets or real-world tasks to validate the method’s generalizability?</strong></em></p>
<p>Thank the reviewer for this suggestion. We finetuned EfficientFormer-L1 on Places-365 datasets, which contains 1.8M images and is more challenging than ImageNet (models often perform worse on Places-365 than ImageNet).</p>

<p>The table above demonstrates that INSTANT remains highly effective even with large datasets such as Places-365. All three versions of INSTANT show only a minor reduction in performance compared to vanilla training, despite achieving a threefold reduction in both computational cost and memory usage. When compared to LBP-WHT, INSTANT offers slightly better performance while maintaining the same memory and computational budget. In contrast, Gradient Filtering fails to converge, yielding poor results. Thank the Reviewer for this suggestion. We added these experiments to our main paper <em>(Appendix L.3, line 1397).</em></p>

background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="k7rtD5fwcS"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="parent-title"><h5><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-share-alt " aria-hidden="true"></span> Replying to (Continue) Restatement of existing experiments and extra baselines for comparisons (Response 2/n)</h5></div><div class="heading"><h4><strong>Analysis of Calibration overhead and Restatement of existing experiments (Response 3/n)</strong></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制笔记 k7rtD5fwcS 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note k7rtD5fwcS"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note k7rtD5fwcS</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>21 Nov 2025, 03:28 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:38)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=k7rtD5fwcS">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p><em><strong>→ W3.1. Unaddressed Limitations: The paper does not sufficiently explore some of the limitations of the proposed method, including the computational cost of SVD.</strong></em></p>
<p>We propose a calibration-based algorithm for constructing the projection, specifically designed to mitigate the computational cost of SVD. Importantly, under our calibration scheme, SVD is performed only during the calibration phase, and within each calibration, it is applied at most once per layer, thereby only introducing a small computational cost. The details of this procedure are provided in Appendix B.3 <em>(line 762)</em>, in which we proved the calibration cost to be 1/40 computational cost of backpropagation.</p>
<p>Considering our targets of reducing both peak memory and FLOPs, our calibration needs to be efficient:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Calibration does not increase peak memory</strong> thanks to on-policy processing several batches at once, and a smaller batch setup (Appendix B.3, <em>line 766</em>).</li>
<li><strong>Small calibration computation</strong> thanks to long-term subspaces utilization, which is proved via the following analysis of the ratio between calibration FLOP <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="212" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D453 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D459 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45C TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45D TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D450 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> and training FLOP <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="213" style="font-size: 121.3%; </ul>
<p><strong>Proof of small computation overhead of calibration:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Experiment Setup:</strong> 5 calibration iterations for each <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="214" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D441 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.085em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> training iteration (as in our experiments). </ul>
<p><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">因此，校准的 FLOP 比训练的 FLOP 小（例如，在</font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">主论文的表 1</font></font><em><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">第 340 行中，INSTANT-5 在 EfficientFormerL1 上训练需要</font></font></em><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"></font><strong><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">475</font></font></strong><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"> MFLOP，校准还</font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">需要额外的</font></font><strong><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">37 MFLOP）。</font></font></strong><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"></font></p>
<p><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">因此，仅报告训练 FLOPs 仍然能够与其他方法进行公平的比较。然而，校准 FLOPs 虽然很小，但不可忽略。感谢审稿人的建议。我们已将此分析添加到正文中，以使我们的方法更加清晰</font></font><em><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">（第 810 行）</font></font></em><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">。</font></font></p>

This progress has led to a significant increase in its complexity. However, despite extensive research on accelerating inference, training deep models directly within a resource-constrained budget remains a considerable challenge due to its high computational and memory requirements. In this paper, we introduce INSTANT (compressIng gradieNtS and acTivAtions for resource-efficieNt Training), a method designed to address both the computational and the memory bottlenecks when training. INSTANT reduces resource demands during backpropagation by projecting gradients and activations into a low-rank subspace and performing computation within that compressed representation. Experimental results demonstrate that INSTANT achieves a $15\\\\times$ reduction in computational cost and $32\\\\times$ reduction in activation memory with negligible impact on model performance. Compressing Gradients and Activations for Resource-Efficient Training | OpenReview\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"1\",{\"name\":\"description\",\"content\":\"Deep learning has advanced at an unprecedented pace. This progress has led to a significant increase in its complexity. However, despite extensive research on accelerating inference, training deep models directly within a resource-constrained budget remains a considerable challenge due to its high computational and memory requirements. In this paper, we introduce INSTANT (compressIng gradieNtS and acTivAtions for resource-efficieNt Training), a method designed to address both the computational and the memory bottlenecks when training. INSTANT reduces resource demands during backpropagation by projecting gradients and activations into a low-rank subspace and performing computation within that compressed representation. Experimental results demonstrate that INSTANT achieves a $15\\\\times$ reduction in computational cost and $32\\\\times$ reduction in activation memory with negligible impact on model performance. The code will be made publicly available upon the paper's acceptance.\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"2\",{\"name\":\"citation_title\",\"content\":\"INSTANT: Compressing Gradients and Activations for Resource-Efficient Training\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"3\",{\"name\":\"citation_author\",\"content\":\"Tuan-Kiet Doan\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"4\",{\"name\":\"citation_author\",\"content\":\"Trung-Hieu Tran\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"5\",{\"name\":\"citation_author\",\"content\":\"Enzo Tartaglione\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"6\",{\"name\":\"citation_author\",\"content\":\"Nikola Simidjievski\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"7\",{\"name\":\"citation_author\",\"content\":\"Van-Tam Nguyen\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"8\",{\"name\":\"citation_online_date\",\"content\":\"2025/10/08\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"9\",{\"name\":\"citation_pdf_url\",\"content\":\"https://openreview.net/pdf?id=P2q6Y7UweV\"}],[\"$\",\"meta\",\"10\",{\"name\":\"citation_abstract\",\"content\":\"Deep learning has advanced at an unprecedented pace. This progress has led to a significant increase in its complexity. However, despite extensive research on accelerating inference, training deep models directly within a resource-constrained budget remains a considerable challenge due to its high computational and memory requirements. In this paper, we introduce INSTANT (compressIng gradieNtS and acTivAtions for resource-efficieNt Training), a method designed to address both the computational and the memory bottlenecks when training. INSTANT reduces resource demands during backpropagation by projecting gradients and activations into a low-rank subspace and performing computation within that compressed representation.
